# Integration of Knowledge Management and Web 3.0 for Human Resource Management

Pradeep BediS. B. GoyalJugnesh KumarRitika

#### Abstract

Knowledge management (KM) is a technique that is used to develop and manage organizational performance in such a competitive scenario due to current fundamental economic resources. This chapter is aimed at analyzing KM linked to decision support systems for human resource management to argue the relevance of knowledge in organizations. The implementation of KM has increased rapidly with the development of Web 3.0\. So, the management information systems (MIS) of an organization must be upgraded and information stored in it can be made available easily to those who need it. The proposed framework will deploy KM to achieve high performance and to achieve a sustainable advantage. This framework will focus on the organization’s functional structure, operational and marketing aspects, and technical aspects.

Keywords: Knowledge Management, Knowledge Resource, Decision-Making System,

## 1 Introduction

In an organization, human resources are considered to be one of the most prominent parts that are needed to be monitored for growth and development. Therefore it is considered to be a typical management task. Management of human resources is a hectic task that includes functions such as hiring staff for the organization, tracking employees’ performance, developing employees’ skills, compensation of employee efforts, and providing requirements of employees. In human resource management (HRM) adequate tools and techniques are also required same as in another domain, to resolve the tasks that can reduce the human efforts and time consumption with accuracy. HRM uses a broad set of heterogeneous tools and techniques to improvise and professionalize entire work. Apart from HRM tools and techniques, other tools from other domain are also adopted as they are required for the analytical process of information such as psychometric analytical tools which are used to analyze the sentimental and emotional state of employees. So, apart from data analytics, this is one of the important working parts of HRM that can be successfully achieved from information systems using Web 3.0 [[1](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_001), [2](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_002), [3](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_003), [4](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_004), [5](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_005)].

Another important uprising tool is artificial intelligence (AI) or machine learning that can be applied in HRM. In this chapter different AI approaches for knowledge management (KM) using AI or machine learning are discussed. There are a large set of heterogeneous AI techniques to handle specific HRM tasks such as product improvement in industries, smart employee selection process, smart employee recruitment process, and performance evaluation of employees. These contributions have given a broad insight into the potential of AI techniques to explore HRM tasks and to improvise their efficiency. Thus, this chapter explores the concept of Web 3.0 and its applications in KM as well as technologies such as AI and cloud computing and their proficiency in HR management. In [Section 2](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_s_002) overview of KM is discussed with their application areas. [Section 3](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_s_003) discusses the enhanced information management (IM) for understanding issues of IM and their enhancements. [Section 4](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_s_004) describes an overview of Web 3.0 with their needs and objectives with their tools. [Section 5](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_s_005) describes the application of Web 3.0 in HRM in the current scenario. As in [Section 6](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_s_006) KM and its integration are discussed. In [Section 7](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_s_007), the application of AI for KM is discussed. In [Section 8](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_s_008), after analyzing the drawbacks of the existing system, a framework for HRM integrated with the machine learning approach and cloud computing is proposed. Finally, in [Section 9](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_s_009) conclusion and future research scope of this chapter are described.

## 2 Overview of knowledge management

To have success in this real world two things are very crucial for the company: the first is innovation and the second one is performance; to stay for the long run in the global market. For that, it is very important to stay more strongly than ever before to meet the customer’s satisfaction, thereby resulting in the increase of productivity and competition. Following the changes in the field of technology must also be needed to maintain an adequate contribution of every tool as well as techniques along with the accessibility of information [6]. This knowledge was diversified, delocalized, and not easily managed, leading to the development of several methods for controlling information systems to leverage it as soon as possible. This information is currently processed and handled by taking its context and its semantics into account, which means that we handle awareness. Nowadays, information is seen as a strategic resource and a stability element, giving it a decisive competitive advantage. To innovate in products, procedures, services, and the organization, KM is essential for the business. It allows its design costs, development, delivery, and so on to be minimized at the same time. Managing understanding is not a new problem. The difference from the past is that businesses are now explicitly attacking this topic according to an aware, regulated, and voluntary approach [[7](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_007)]. In the past, this was done without thinking about it. KM appeared as a discipline in response to a wide range of problems arising from memory losses due to retirement departures, technological growth, and innovation. To improve the added value of design and development methods, it is seen as a practice that needs particular approaches. To improvise the skills and to increase the profits of an organization, different companies adopt different strategies. All strategies are designed to maintain a proper place or rank in the market. Approaches must be different, but these all ultimately lead to the profit earning for the organization. However, for designing these strategies, KM is quite necessary that leads to the exponential increment of profit concerning investment cost. So, KM can be explored differently and dynamically. KM will lead to handling long-term challenges such as capitalization, team co-ordination, recruitments, retirements, performance evaluation, and project management. This can be achieved from past learning and their deployment in present conditions to achieve the dedicated mission and vision of an organization. In current competitive world, IM guarantees the company’s survival [[8](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_008)].

As a consequence of the rapid changes in the business environment today, the need for IM has increased. Second, customization systems require knowledge of different consumer needs and expectations. Second, KM, which involves codification, personalization, and information process controls, requires numerous end-use technology implementations and acceleration of technological change. Third, the increasing variety of information sources from the increased use of value-chain outsourcing and deconstruction demands that these increased sources be controlled. In recent years, IM has gained widespread attention. In the current competitive environment, the value of information is of the highest priority in every field, be it the industrial or the educational field. There is a huge contribution of researchers to explore the advantages of KM for understanding the information value of an organization. So, to further explore the advantages of KM it is quite required to first understand the need, objectives, and tools of KM in the exploration of knowledge of organizations. In this section, definition and principles of knowledge management (KM) is summarized [[9](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_009)].

A multitude of interpretations and meanings of information and KM exist. It is important to identify them before proceeding further:

*   Knowledge is a whole collection of customer-related instincts, logic, observations, interactions, goods, procedures, economies, competitiveness, and so on that make for successful action.

*   Knowledge management is a structured, coordinated, clear, and intentional mechanism in which knowledge is developed, disseminated, implemented, refreshed, and modified to achieve organizational goals.

Two main facets are required to understand the definition of KM is such as:

*   Treating the information aspect of company operations as an explicit market issue reflected at all levels of the enterprise in strategy, practice, and policy.

*   Establishing a clear correlation between the intellectual capabilities of an entity, both overt and implicit, and development. In reality, KM also includes defining and mapping intellectual properties within the enterprise in terms of these two factors, creating new expertise within the organization for strategic advantage, making large quantities of organizational information available, exchanging best practices, and technology that makes all of the above.

Unless functions of KM are specified, its utilities cannot be observed. To understand the functioning of KM it is required to understand their values in order to withstand in this competitive scenario. These values are discussed as below:

*   The most important value of KM is awareness that needed to be connected. In perceptions and insights, it is the collective knowledge and its value stems from its relational relationships and qualities surrounding its content.

*   Compatibility of KM with this dynamically changing world is also another important value that needed to be achieved. This can be resolved by integration awareness about new information, competition in market as well as development.

*   It is termed as catalyst which improves the activities or actions of an organization. For this value awareness is also important as it tracks the environmental conditions and tracks the dynamic development of the market.

*   The solution of KM will also contribute for information sharing. The variation in this concept will inform the organization about the changing activities of market and competition.

Along with these values, different analysts have set up their theories to establish relationship between KM and IM [[10](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_010)].

## 3 Enhanced information management

To study the enhancement of KM, first of all, it is needed to understand the relationship between IM and KM. As in an organization, several pieces of information, in the form of emails, manuals, surveys, and so on, are being performed to improve and make the expertise more analytical. As it is a quite difficult and hectic task to share knowledge individually due to various reasons such as restriction of time and geographical location a collective automated platform is required for all individuals working in an organization. Therefore, to achieve expertise analysis of KM, it is quite necessary to integrate with IM.

This information directs the employee to the appropriate source of expertise; it doesn’t matter if it is a text or an excerpt. These maps of organizational information or directories of expertise identify a collection of categories of knowledge, the position of the knowledge, and, in some cases, its state and value. It exists as an entity to fulfill various unique tasks and goals. With improvement or enhancement of information systems, information use and reuse can be successfully performed by expertise, which is encouraged by KM. This complex distinction between KM and IM focuses on the related component of information as a vital distinguishing attribute [[14](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_014), [15](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_015), [16](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_016), [17](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_017), [18](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_018)].

## 4 Overview of Web 3.0

While understanding the features of Web 3.0, it is necessary to understand the transition of Web 1.0 to Web 2.0 and from Web 2.0 to Web 3.0\. It is because all features of Web 1.0 are integrated into Web 2.0 as well as all features of Web 2.0 are contributed into Web 3.0 ([Figure 1](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_fig_001)). In terms of information, in the Web 2.0 era, several networking networks like Blogger, Twitter, or Facebook have been created and the sharing of data between multiple users has shifted. The new Website update, known as Web 3.0, incorporates the characteristics of both phases with some more features ([Figure 1](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_fig_001)). By Web 3.0 we mean an alleged third wave of Internet implementations, which together constitute what could be referred to as “smart web” including semantic web users, microformats, natural-language analysis, data storage, computer learning, AI technology. “Web 3.0 is the third generation of Internet-based web services that emphasize machine-facilitated knowledge of the information to provide a more productive and intuitive user experience,” says Wikipedia. Web 3.0 is one of the leading technology for digital era as stated by Nova Spivack, CEO of Radar Networks. (2010–2020) [[19](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_019), [20](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_020)].

In other words, the third wave of the Internet that is made accessible by a mixture of different new technologies can be explained as follows [[21](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_021)]:

*   Transforming the site from a transparent and interoperable network with diverse apps and content repositories.

*   Universal networking, cable network penetration, and smartphone Internet usage.

*   Network computing, business models for SaaS, online service interoperability, remote computing, cloud computing, and fog computing.

*   Open technology and frameworks along with APIs and protocols.

*   Open identification, open credibility, smartphone identity roaming, and personal details.

*   Smart mobile, web semantic technology.

*   The “World Wide Database” distributed database.

*   Smart software, natural language, artificial learning, machine thinking, and automated agents.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-005_fig_001.png)

Figure 1: Evolution of web.

### 4.1 Need for Web 3.0

In Web 1.0 or 2.0 when anything is searched over the Internet then it will display some website links that may or may not be acceptable by an individual. Then in such a condition he/she has to enter different keywords to access the appropriate information. So, such web searching is dependent on keywords. Such implementation takes a quite long time to get the appropriate result. So, there is a requirement for improvement in the searching capability of web applications that can take less time to give a better solution. Web 3.0 overcomes this challenge when it lets the user get the answer he needs. Network 3.0 is a way to fix the issues described above. The whole quest mechanism can be shifted as platform 3.0 is integrated into the system. This will help to gather information easily from the Internet. By searching, arranging, and only presenting the pertinent information the system can be better equipped to provide the user with the information needed. An “age of artificial intelligence” can be called the Web 3.0 era.

### 4.2 Fundamentals of Web 3.0

Certain analysts say that Web 3.0 has almost every piece of information about a remote wizard. This is why it is often referred to as a “giant archive.” Web 2.0 uses the Internet to link people, while Web 3.0 is used for knowledge sharing Web 2.0\. The following example will help illustrate this. You need to look at these places using the latest apps online to get the information you need on travel and sites, visitor destinations, and so on if you want to get a holiday with an approximate budget of $3,000\. The prices of various travel agencies can be compared by visiting numerous websites. This is undoubtedly a time-consuming and boring approach. But the time spent browsing multiple sites can be reduced with Web 3.0, so the Internet does all this, through narrowly defined search parameters. The Web browser can compile, interpret, and display the data in a manner that makes it easy to compare. This happens as the information available on the web is understandable by Web 3.0\. A Web 3.0 search engine scans not only to search for the given keywords but also interprets their meaning. It can include related findings along with recommendations for more data searching. The “Internet of services” is often referred to as Network 3.0\. In addition to the human-readable web portion, open machine resources can be merged into higher service standards.

### 4.3 Structure of Web 3.0

In contrast to the previous ITs of the Internet, Platform 3.0 has a new GUI. Semiotics and semantics describe the fundamental framework. Semantics refers to communication meaning, while semi-organisms are the study of symbol processes and signals (semiotics), both independently and organized into sign or context and communication structures. It needs to analyze how meaning is formed and understood. Semantics can benefit the consumer very well, as it allows him to get the right answer.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-005_fig_002.png)

Figure 2: Objectives and components of Web 3.0.

### 4.4 Advantages of Web 3.0

Improves data management: Data administration on the Internet is a complex activity since it requires the interpretation of many programs for the convergence of the data of various systems that can or may not be interpreted by a computer in certain cases. In these cases, the relation of data to the appropriate input is very difficult. This dilemma can be solved to a degree by using the semantic web if it tells the mutual understanding between the various data sets, making it easier for the system to understand and efficiently combine connections with the desired output.

Mobile accessibility: Overall, by the end of 2008, more than 4 billion mobile device users was anticipated. The worldwide smartphone penetration rates were expected to exceed 50% at the same time. But with the launch of the 3G technology, this number was projected to hit 60%, making it easier to access the Internet on handheld devices. As a result, the market for Web 3.0 is projected to grow due to its excellent performance. Internet access via mobile devices is supposed to have an important part to play. The size of the web page [22–24] in Web 3.0 can be reduced by the application of CSS.

Creativity tools: The multiple links between separate databases is the main feature on Platform 3.0\. Structures and individuals will use the data and information datasets in the same manner and thereby boost their effectiveness. This facilitates innovative processes, which in turn lead to the development of a new model for ideas and R&D.

Globalization: Network 3.0, with a common programming language called the resource definition framework (RDF), is attempting to construct and standardize various data structures. This is the underlying data exchange model of the network. RDF is written in XML such that different programs can easily exchange details. This breaks constraints on latency, poor web view on mobile devices, and so on.

Enhanced customers’ satisfaction: Network 3.0 gives the corporation “artificial intelligence” to increase the degree of customer service satisfaction (CSS). Via this, consumers can view the full product information on their webpage. This increases the reputation of the company on the market, leading to more customers. Some of the technologies employed in Web 3.0 are illustrated in [Figure 3](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_fig_003).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-005_fig_003.png)

Figure 3: Technologies of Web 3.0.

## 5 Web 3.0 and HRM

Each field is being changed by launching a better plan that improves with the matter of time. The convention of a particular system has been enhanced by Internet of Things (IoT). An example of frond end applications of IoT is smart gadgets that are adaptable sensor by using its features. Periodically, the gadgets of IoT with the capacitance of heterogeneous billions there is implementation and constraints quality need of quality of service (QoS). HRM has grown to the advance to all of things movement, business capacity, heterogeneous data support, drawing practicality, data integration, and high relativity in the year 2020 [[25](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_025)].

The tools and services provided by the network infrastructure and the Internet will be accessed by human resources professionals. In the furtherance of Internet software, the concepts Web 1.0, Web 2.0, and Web 3.0 have typically been accomplished in many stages. Web 3.0 was planned as the development of high-quality goods and services by experts using Web 2.0 technology as a feasible platform, by Jason Calacanis in 2007. The true implementation of technologies and software devices started in 2010\. There is no competition in a useful interpretation of instances of the web 3.0\. Therefore, many mobile or smart applications used the concept of web 3.0 to deliver better video quality, machine control tools and many more 3-D effects including augmented reality [[26](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_026)].

In current internet scenario it is quite difficult to distinguish between Web 2.0 and Web 3.0\. The introduction of Web 3.0 or Web 2.0 in human resource management systems (HRMS) is in wide application for human capital management, workforce management, talent development system, business learning systems, e-recruitment, etc.

With application of Web 2.0 or Web 3.0 technologies for applications in HRM, this would motivate the participation of users to interact with information and enhance services for HR professionals. To support this, there are already many HRMS applications are available [[27](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_027)].

Currently, many smart applications are also available as a separate online HR support. In the HR management, there are scope for research opportunities for developing tools and techniques with integration of Web 3.0.

## 6 Knowledge management and integration (KMI)

In the last two decades, KM has gained a lot of attention to sustain in this competitive world. This has increased the ability of an organization to focus and embed specialist knowledge to improvise their outcomes. Knowledge Management and Innovative technologies had shown up the proficiency for the organization in terms of profit and satisfaction level [[28](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_028)]. After studying the previous contributions of researchers in the field of HRM, KM is considered to be the most important pillar for information extraction and their management processes such as knowledge acquisition, sharing, transfer, and integration. A general social scenario is created by the HRM practices which move in the direction of proficient knowledge integration. Before understanding the relation between HRM and KI, it is important to know about goals ([Figure 4](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_fig_004)).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-005_fig_004.png)

Figure 4: Goals of KI.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-005_fig_005.png)

Figure 5: System model of KI.

In recent studies [[29](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_029), [30](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_030), [31](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_031), [32](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_032), [33](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_033)] relationship between HRM and KM processes have been explored along with performance results. So, these studies have improvised the understanding of the role of KM in every field. It is also known how the HRM is influenced by the KM for other firms such as outsourcing firms. This shows the raised importance of KM from the last two decades which helps in creating a knowledge base for the deployment of new products and processes that contribute to increment of productivity as well as profit ([Figure 5](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_fig_005)).

## 7 Contextualization of knowledge management using AI

Suriyan Jomthanachai et al. [[34](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_034)] showed the HRM application in rubberwood factory for converting it from industry to industry 4.0\. The main objective of conversion was the large number of production data that are quite difficult to handle manually. This causes incorrect compilation of data and delayed services. This serious issue faced by the company also affected the inventory management as well as salary payment of employees. This ultimately leads to economic issues for the company along with the labor-intensive tasks. Then to resolve these issues, the application of machine learning (genetic algorithm (GA)) was used. The application of GA checked utilization of raw materials and also performed the data analysis task with efficiency. A web-based application was also developed to manage such large production data and analysis to enhance the productivity of the entire system timely.

Anna Sołtysik-Piorunkiewicz et al. [[35](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_035)] studied the usefulness of web technologies and their support systems to manage data in organizations. The application of KM was also studied in this work. In this study, a detailed discussion for stages of KM was discussed such as generation, collection, analysis, and evaluation of information or knowledge for application with new-era web technologies. The technique was focused on the structural, functional, technical, and analytical aspects.

Maria Dominic et al. [[36](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_036)] discussed technologies and features of Web 3.0 such as semantic web, big data, cloud computing, and augmented and virtual realities. The study states that the development of Web 3.0 has moved from passive learning to active learning technologies. In this work, the e-learning area was focused on observing the impact of Web 3.0 features and technologies and along with that different issues associated with Web 3.0 were also studied.

Prof. Mohamed M. El Hadi et al. [[37](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_037)] discussed the features of Web 3.0 such as semantic web, big data, cloud computing, and AI. Author discussed the contribution of AI and semantic web for successful and efficient knowledge management. In this work, educational KM is taken as the domain to show the proficiency of Web 3.0 with semantic web and AI features. This makes the process of learning quite varying from traditional processes normally utilized in teaching and learning. This work presents KM systems prototype in educational organizations by utilizing Web 3.0 technologies.

Amit Sheth et al. [[38](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_038)] discussed methods to utilize all types of modern web resources utilizing the key principles of the capability to relate meaning with data via conceptual or domain models and semantic details consisting of annotations, and through advanced semantic techniques for search, integration, and analysis. This work talks about the utilization of semantic web standards and methods but also supports the utilization of lighter-weight, convenient, and more scalable choices when they are more convenient.

Robin D. Morris et al. [[39](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_039)] discussed about the impact of Web 3.0, semantic web, on the e-learning platform for its development. It is said that the online users must have the knowledge of Web 3.0 technologies for upcoming advancements in the field of e-Learning. This work gives an illustrative understanding of semantic web and its features as the basics of semantic web are unification of data. In Web 3.0, by utilizing the metadata data is transformed into significant details that can be accessed and analyzed. This work helps the online users for e-learning such as tutors, for evaluation of course and in learning.

Víctor Hugo Medina García et al. [[40](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_040)] discussed the association and interconnection between complicated theory and KM. Planning was done to find processes to exchange or share knowledge effectively, upgrading KM in a complicated organization like the evaluation of software factories. As a consequence, it gives a prototype of KM, considering the concept of complexity theory employed to a formulation of system software factories, where usual formulation requires various types of complications supervised properly.

C. Rosa Yeh et al. [[41](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_041)] discussed that how technology had changed the process of acquiring skills. This work was carried out to assist entrepreneurs to better understand the varying technologies and its impact on society. From the point of connectivism, this work is intended to integrate the advanced learning approach. Connectivism study includes the social interaction technologies and implementation of KM. Empirical overview questionnaires were redirected to 301 industries in the computer firm covering Taiwan, out of which 80 authentic feedbacks were obtained. The hypothesis was proposed that proved the direct impacts of corporate culture on organizational learning. Newly established industries show comparatively elevated enthusiasm for innovation, learning, and its adoption of modern technologies.

Tsang-Kai Chang et al. [[42](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_042)] demonstrated the hypothesis to project and understand actions as well as behaviors over the Internet. The e-learning domain was taken as an example. In this work, experimental analysis was performed to extract the impact of users or contestants to improve the usage caliber over the e-learning platform. The experiment was performed on 176 educational administrators. A small opinion polling was performed to understand the “superior effect,” “peer effect,” and “regulations.” The result analysis showed up benefits of “peer effect”. So, it also demonstrated that rules have not much impact. The co-relation analysis was also performed over these effects and the outcome shows that almost all contestants are affected by peers on utilizing e-learning.

Luz Andrea Rodríguez Rojas et al. [[43](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_043)] discussed that health tourism is a popular sector in many countries in which industrialized countries are delivering improved, cheaper medical care. The above model is used for the management of expertise and is also focused on information and communication technology (ICT) in the medical tourism field. This model presented a tool for achieving upgraded decision-making process of the business as it contributes to knowledge, value, and productivity of an expertise. This management model provides a roadmap for these goals by developing executing instruments.

Suhaila Samsur et al. [[44](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_044)] discussed about gaining accurate knowledge to create a secure electronic medical record (EMR) system for security analysis of shared information. Previously, a preliminary survey of interviews was done with a chosen group of HIS developers. The result analysis showed that four factors are needed to be considered for designing privacy preserving framework for hospital information system (HIS). These factors are such as, legislation, ethical, technology and cultural. With application of KM, more systematic rules can be retrieved to achieve security of an information. Additionally, it envisioned light for designing more secure HIS.

Anna Závodská et al. [[45](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_045)] analyzed the role of KM and their process along with performance. The designed architecture in this work showed up the importance of each level of knowledge information. Comparison of knowledge is also discussed about different firms. Marketing campaign information is given. The method involves three case studies, which collect and analyze facts. The study indicates that the framework of the value-added mechanism can be used as an estimation method for analysis of values in many phases and different business management methods are appropriate. Proposed modifications are made on the grounds of the questions discussed. The blue ocean strategy principles are the basis for these recommendations. Blue ocean technology is considered to be a better approach to knowledge processing and the advancement of the organizations.

Fernando J. Garrigos-Simon et al. [[46](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_046)] considered the developments and transformations faced by ICTs in the development of the social networks and Web 3.0 technology in the contemporary economic and social environment because of the effects of recent innovations on corporate efficiency. The inquiry illustrates the use of social networks and the growth of digital business models associated with them, which highlight the importance of group managers and crowdsource platforms. In light of recent progress, the report explores possible sources of competitive gains for businesses and describes the improvements they should bring to improve decision-making and use current situations. The paper analyzes the impact of social network management and Platform 3.0 technology promotion and discusses methods for strengthening competitive advantages.

Muhammad Waseem Muhammad Zia et al. [[47](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_047)] discussed about third generation of Internet, Web 3.0\. It is used to create external data storage facilities to allow any kind of data to be exchanged across any network. This covers the hypothesis that in the third century the location and the computer sciences were associated with the library. Various advantages of modern web technologies, Web 3.0, have been established to develop applications for library information science.

A huge number of scientists, researchers, and so on are involved in Network 3.0\. Semantic platform systems allow consistent knowledge from various domains to be combined to reach special solutions. Currently, semantic web applications are not as popular as other types of enterprise services, so their early adopters can be encouraged by defining their consumer abilities. Ilona Pawełoszek et al. [[48](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_048)] introduced semantic web software characteristics. Some indications have been provided of the strategic position of semantic pharmaceutical and e-commerce applications.

Rajiv et al. [[49](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_049)] discussed that Web 3.0 have assisted in web-based learning that assists people in reading and writing. Web 2.0 and Web 3.0 have created innovations with a much shorter period, which allow for simple web-based schooling and learning. The development and functionality of Web 3.0 are defined in these papers. There will also be talks about possible future innovations, trends, applications, and so on.

Ju-Young M. Kang et al. [[50](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_050)] focused on whether (1) (a) economic tenderness, (b) conformity with the m-Internet equipment, and (c) leisure patterns lead to conditional rewards, social self-concept profits, emotional advantages, and functional benefits.

Pranita Barpute et al. [[51](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_051)] discussed that e-commerce is one of the most popular industries today. We add, remove, change, and update items to suit the demands of dealers and end customers in online shopping. The search article would be easier to run for users accessing the online shopping structure provided the computer has the correct user interface. This will help various distributors grow and bind them to their brand online. For the proposed platform, the semantic web and Web 3.0 can even be used to help users and also distributors. The interfaces for both buyers and dealers are shown.

Daisy Wadhwa et al. [[52](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_052)] discussed that the standard of living is completely changed by technology. In the current world, the accessibility of technologies of web provide us the way to establish path for collaborative learning. Collaborative learning is trending among all varieties of learning. This study spots the benefits and aims of collaborative learning online. The implementing outline of Web 3.0 from Web 1.0 is available by this study. This study spots the aim and benefits of online collaborative learning. The implementing outline of Web 3.0 from Web 1.0 is also discussed in this study. This study also provides insights about tools of Web 3.0 that makes the learners to collaborate among them and to learn and teach simultaneously. Then a review is also presented for “Wiki-Learning” as collaborative learning platform.

Many organizations are offering to install KM that has become very popular, for enhancing the performance of their organization. Hayfa. Y. Abuaddous et al. [[53](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_053)] inspected the literature for showing the effects of management of knowledge and some of its practices on organizations performance. According to the observations, the efficiency of KM is identified the direct or indirect relation with organization’s performance.

Testing is a dominant activity for incrementing the quality of product and original information support. KM connected with the process of testing increments the capacity in identification and defects removing, coupling the area information and technology in the implementation of the process are discussed by Darío Enrique Soto Duran et al. [[54](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_054)]. The dominant ingredient for any organization desiring competitive benefits is the efficient management of the knowledge. This study describes that KM is the primary characteristic of the performance of an organization and the dominant tool for the proper running of an organization. So the organization has to accept the full advantage of knowledge value for managing, creating, applying knowledge effectively, and sharing. Taking care of three main components – processes, technology, and people – is required for managing the knowledge efficiently. Consequently creating, managing, sharing and utilizing knowledge effectively is quite important for an organization to utilize the advantages of KM was discussed by Funmilola Olubunmi Omotay.

Bahman Aghamirian et al. [[56](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_056)] studied the relationship between organizational behaviour and customer’s knowledge management in e-commerce and envisioned 8 factors such as: knowledge for the customers, knowledge from customers, knowledge about customers, recording and spreading customers’ knowledge, quality of the services and products, reasonable prices and satisfaction of customers. People who were all proficient and experts of organizational market of Mellat Bank were selected and 210 proficient who are working in companies of Iranian insurance for the statistic society were selected.

Diana Elena Ranf et al. [[57](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_ref_057)] demonstrated the needs and requirement for the structured framework to cluster and relief the information reuse and new knowledge production in managing projects. These necessities are fulfilled by KM and gives way for enhancing the practices of project management. This paper covers a notice of profits and rewards obtained by developers by installing and accepting the strategy of project management. A great role of the paper is to build a way to use the management of knowledge in project management. This begins from the four stages of the KM cycle also installed in project management.

## 8 Proposed framework

HRM is a department in all organizations that can see and handle all requirements and aspects of employees. There are several jobs that HRM handles such as employee hiring, their job performance analysis, and benefits appraisal. HRM department is dedicated to the welfare of employees in an organization. The existing HRM approach has the following problems:

*   Management of organizational knowledge compatibility with Web 3.0.

*   Communication overheads among groups and individuals.

*   Inefficient documentation and proper sharing of information among employees.

While keeping these in mind, the proposed framework is designed to coordinate the entire HRM system globally. The proposed framework is designed for using a machine learning approach over the cloud. The framework is designed in the distributed architecture described below ([Figure 6](#9783110722642_006797.xhtml_b_9783110722789-005_fig_006)).

The following steps are performed for proposed architecture:

1.  The data collection unit collects the data from employees regarding their complaints, performance, appraisal, bonus distribution, etc.

2.  Then the collected data is stored at the cloud server from where other units can access data.

3.  Then analysis unit integrated with machine learning will perform analysis according to the requirement.

4.  The result of the analysis is also stored at the cloud server.

5.  Finally, decision-making unit is integrated with a machine learning approach such as fuzzy logic to decide for employees and to coordinate to them through a cloud server.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-005_fig_006.png)

Figure 6: Proposed architecture for HRM integrated with ML over cloud.

Key features of the proposed architecture are:

1.  In this research work, machine learning/AI is used to collect, analyze, and decisions making for employees. This will show the integration of Web 3.0 features (AI and cloud computing) for making advancement in HRM procedure.

2.  KM or data mining approach is used for analytical purposes.

3.  This model is quite efficient for finding the best solution for problems existing in the organization.

4.  This decision support system also efficient with respect to time.

5.  This model gives decisions without any partiality and indirectly helps in the development of the organization.

## 9 Conclusion

In this chapter, the impact of the KM evaluation with Web 3.0 usability for organizational knowledge generation, sharing, organizing, and their leverage in HRM is discussed. In this chapter, by using the advantages of Web 3.0 tools and techniques such as AI and cloud computing, the proposed framework is designed to make the smart HRM system supporting collaborative learning in employees’ growth in an organization. The proposed framework will prove its feasibility of KM concerning organizational growth. As part of the continuity of this work, the experimental implementation of smart HRM is planned to enhance their efficiency level.
