# Education 4.0 and Web 3.0 Applications in Enhancing Learning Management System: Post-Lockdown Analysis in COVID-19 Pandemic

Sandhya JavaHiba MohammedAradhana Balodi BhardwajVinod Kumar Shukla

#### Abstract

Education 4.0 is a new vision for learning that enables educators and apprentices to align technology with research to discover new possibilities. The advancements made through the Internet, artificial intelligence, and virtual classrooms have drastically transformed the education industry. The onset of novel coronavirus forced the whole world into immediate lockdown, with schools and universities shifting to digital platforms to keep the learning process going. The pandemic affected about 90% of the student population globally and forced educational institutions to consider unconventional approaches for teaching. The modifications made to the existing system for the pandemic depended on resources derived from Education 4.0 which could be permanent. Many of these were technological upgrades to the current mode, focused more on student’s development and learning from the initial stages, along with plans for more differentiated and inclusive lessons. However, the changes made currently or in the future would be long term which could have its challenges and advantages and require extensive planning through research for smooth implementation. **Web 3.0** assets derived from data integration have improved e-learning opportunities for students through personalization, knowledge management, and enhanced information delivery while providing assistance for instructors in course development, record keeping, and teaching support. Initially, this research investigates what are the new tools and technology being applied to the current structure. This chapter aims to examine the impact of these practices introduced as part of Education 4.0 on learning, post-COVID-19 for students and teachers. Highlighting the updates in Education 4.0 from previous revolutions has been strongly considered in this study due to the lack of awareness.

The chapter carefully analyzes existing literature on the trends of Education 4.0 extracted from the Industrial Revolution (IR) 4.0 initiated in 2012\. A review was conducted to understand the correlation between IR 4.0 and **Web 3.0** practices being used for teaching students at different levels during the outbreak. An empirical-analytical methodology was undertaken to gather data through an online questionnaire designed for school and university students. The undertaken sample was questioned about their experience with e-learning tools during the pandemic and suggestions were taken for future education mode preferences. Through this investigation, researchers could answer the impending question, can initiating Education 4.0 during this pandemic revolutionize the education system post-COVID and equip students with the skills needed to survive in the twenty-first century. Organizations and the school system need to understand the impact of these modifications on all the members of the learning community from a future perspective. Post-pandemic, researchers could study ways to better implement the specific tools discussed in this chapter and evaluate their effectiveness in different offline classroom environments. The shift to e-learning could be the much-needed change to the global education structure for a more inclusive and student-centered learning environment.

Keywords: Education 4.0, COVID-19, E-Learning, Technology, Web 3.0, Post-pandemic, Education System,

## 1 Introduction

At present, much of what is known to us as the physical world has evolved through big advancements, and developments in different industries, pushing mankind to work more, better and faster. The technological changes in the last few decades have pushed us into an era of digitization with virtual assistants make our lives easier, 3D environments giving experiences like never before and netizens connecting with each other globally. All of the good and bad were possible only due to man’s belief in proving itself as the superior race through revolutions of the industry.

Before the development and introduction of industry 4.0, there were three distinct industrial reforms that changed mankind’s experience of the working environment and paved the way for greater technological shifts in the coming years. The first industrial revolution focused on work through manual labor with aids from animals and basic machine tools. Next leap was the second industrial revolution in the early 1920s with introduction of steel and electricity-powered factories which drastically increased productivity. With the emergence of electronic equipment, the third industrial revolution was pioneered with manufacturers adopting better digital technology and software for their workplace. The growth of Internet and computer technology in industry 3.0 formed the basis of communication and collaboration initiated in the fourth industrial revolution. With access to real time data, cyber physical systems and artificial intelligence the industry has unlocked an advanced level of interconnectivity which empowers businesses to understand and control their operations better than ever before while driving their efficiency [[1](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_001)].

The Industry Revolution 4.0 has been characterized as the fusion of the biological, physical and digital worlds with increasing application of newer technologies like, robotics, big data, 3D printing, artificial intelligence, and Internet of Things (IoT). These advancements have started a process of digitization of not only the business industry but consumers, education, travel, health, and economy as well especially during the COVID-19 pandemic. The fourth Healthcare revolution derived from Industry 4.0 has introduced newer digital tools to automize the health system and prioritize patient care. Like, AI systems assisted hospitals in keeping track of electronic patient data and medical supplies which reduced the staff workload, while Big Data models provided trends and infographics regarding the patients and hospitals in various countries during the pandemic [[2](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_002)]. Similarly, Industry 4.0 also initiated the digital revolution of the education sector known as, “Education 4.0” with learner-focused objectives and technological provisions for every stakeholder (student, teacher, and administration).

Education 1.0 was a system where memory and ample experience were much required with learners being considered to be the receptacles of knowledge. Then, came the Internet with the revolution where pupils broke the tradition and consumed knowledge for their daily life applications which was referred to as the education 2.0\. Here, the apprentices were said to be communicating, collaborating, and co-creating. From there, technologies began more rapid movement. Students started to venture into new types of technologies. Reports suggested that learning should be experienced through collaboration and students should work in the team for production of good learning outcomes and this system was coined to be education 3.0\. Learners were considered to be connectors, creators, and constructivists.

The very next and recent approach was Education 4.0 which aligned with the industrial revolution 4.0\. This model contradicted the previous ideas as knowledge is not just for consumption and production, but innovating diversified ideas with smart technologies, artificial intelligence, and robotics, all of which now deeply impact our everyday lives. Education 4.0 could be called the general upward trend blending the new and existing tools to enhance the education system and prepare students for a future workforce of Industry 4.0 [[3](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_003)]. This system of learning incorporates different technologies and approaches to enable higher educational institutions to align their services and syllabuses to prepare the future graduates for a new advanced beginning.

As the human beings embrace the tech future, how and what we teach in our education system will undergo reshaping, to align the system and keep up to date with the technologies of the twenty-first century. The future of understanding and teaching has taken a great leap from the traditional methods to the novel methods as students give more time and creativity to their class performance using the resources given by the educators while needing lesser guidance. The concept of teachers instructing a room full of students who listen and respond to directions has long become the past of education. While not a complete approach, student learning spaces powered by digital resources will supersede the typical classrooms of today, creating a more personalized learning experience for the students [[4](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_004)], thus aiding students to become partners or co-creators of their own education (shown in [Figure 1](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_fig_001)).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_001.jpg)

Figure 1: Trends of Education 4.0.

Facilitation of experiences that allow collaboration, communication, and teamwork for students needs to be enhanced and strongly considered even outside the classroom. The students shall be allowed to interact in coherence among themselves while working on their own unique learning projects with groups larger or smaller. Classrooms will co-exist as physical spaces and virtual environments while the concept of flipped learning would be strongly considered, so that students can understand theory at home but spend in-class time working as teams to apply their knowledge to real life issues and discussions. The whole idea of learning anytime, anywhere has become crucial due to the current situation and should considered as the “new-normal” due to the pandemic, where social distancing is essential, and schools/universities are virtual. Providing free digital resources and virtual learning environments globally has been an important objective of Education 4.0\. The new tech-driven educational systems of **Education 4.0** also gives an insight for teachers helping them produce individualized lessons for students according to their pace and provide interactive content most beneficial for them. This prepares the learners to become much more innovative, better data collectors, and seize any opportunities that are shared with them [[5](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_005)].

As we ride the wave of digital era, it has become essential to connect with people globally without any barriers. A world of information is at our fingertips, with a click of a button or a voice command. When it comes to digitization, it is crucial that students are provided with new, updated educational systems to cope with being in the world of web. Technology can enhance learning and should be embraced and manipulated for mankind’s advantage. The chapter gives a detailed idea of certain technologies revolutionizing the education industry and how they can be applied for the benefit of various stakeholders in a school or university. Furthermore, a qualitative research design is employed to develop a better understanding of student’s perspective on the Education 4.0 tools and technology during the pandemic.

## 2 Internet of Things (IoT)

IoT is simply the concept of connecting any device that collect, share, and act on information on surrounding the Internet. It covers a wide usage from our home to office apps which include smart and wearable devices and almost anything else that are connected to Internet and communicate with a common language; these objects have built in censors and they come in various shapes and sizes that receive information from the environment and connects to an IoT platform. These platforms integrate and analyze data.

The global population continues to grow and age as people live longer. This calls for innovative ideas to manage the planets resources and to deliver services to everyone. As people shift toward cities the challenges to manage traffic, pollution, and energy increase. With all these going on, individuals have access to information products and global services which are increasing day by day and they expect these to be tailored to their individual needs. Technology is believed to satisfy these needs and contribute to our vision of an augmented life for everyone. Different skills develop solutions to make the world smarter and more connected. Talking about IoT, it is simply the concept of connecting any device that collect, share, and act on information surrounding the Internet. It covers a wide usage from our home to office applications which include smart and wearable devices and almost anything else that are connected to Internet and communicate with a common language [[6](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_006)].

The recent editions of wireless technologies such as embedded sensors and actuator nodes, which gives the opportunity for IoT to transforming the Internet into a fully integrated future network, that receive information from the environment and connects to an IoT platform. The platforms integrate and analyze data and help provide various information about the world around us.

IoT is the term for non-traditional computing devices which are connected to the Internet in order to send data and receive instructions without human interventions. The universal recognition enabled by wireless sensor network (WSN) technologies come across different aspects of our modern-day lifestyles. This helps us in measuring and coming up with an inference which also gives us the opportunity to understand the environmental indicators from delicate ecologies and natural resources to urban environments [[7](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_007)]. The propagation of these high-tech devices in activation of communication network creates the IoT. It influences our daily routine and lifestyle; for example at homes, these smart techniques enable us to allow management of lights, heating, or any home appliance that embeds a smart system, basically giving us an opportunity to use smart home system where we can remotely monitor and control our home. Similar examples of IoT-based technologies would be smart watches that track your daily routine (steps, heart beats, etc.), smart cars, and smart phones. The data securities and secure communications ensure that your personal information always will be safe. Data is emitted from various sensors and IoT platforms receive it and integrate the collected data from various sources; further analytics are performed on the data and valuable information is extracted as per requirement. Finally, the result is shared with other devices for better user experience automation and improving efficiencies. Through these smart technologies IoT helps in redefining our lifestyle and transforming the way we interact with technologies [[8](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_008)].

### 2.1 5G technology with IoT

Fifth-generation wireless or what we currently call the (5G) is the latest reiteration of technology, concocted in order to increase the speed and response of networks, around the world. 5G is an invention which helps enable a sharp intensification in the amount of data transmitted over wireless systems due to more available bandwidth and advanced antenna technology. According to various reports 5G has a multiplied speed which is ten times faster than that of an LTE network. This great upsurge in the transference speed will allow IoT devices to communicate and share data faster. The development of 5G network system along with the IOT, which is considered to be applicable to a variety of promising fields and applications, helps in evolution of various cellular network technologies and that creates a possibility organize immense censors in the framework of IoT and also helps to process data, by challenging all the other technologies of communication or data mining [[9](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_009)].

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_002.jpg)

Figure 2: Development of 5G with IoT.

[Figure 2](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_fig_002) shows an expansion and conjunction of IoT with 5G network and that gives a perfect example of Internet, intelligence, and things. 5G users are no longer just normal smart phone handlers but they are also users of vehicular network, in which case the user interconnects these “vehicles” with others. Moreover, the increase in number of users and the various of the types of users, due to the interoperability with technologies like IoT, results in a diverse form of novel applications, a variety of subjective requirements and poses challenges in being able to understand and satisfy quality of experience (termed as QoE), which focuses on the entire service experience, across the board. Additionally, there are numerous other potential methods and technologies have been proposed, such as millimeter-wave (mm Wave), massive multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO), and machine-to-machine (M2M) communications, so that 5G networks can be well adapted to IoT. To sum up these smart techs help the world to connect with massive devices and make a great contribution to providing advanced and intelligent services for the ease of human beings [[10](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_010)].

### 2.2 Role of IoT in education

Throwing some light on the perspective of education, classrooms are not just confined to lecture halls. As mentioned in the following figure, especially during these times of pandemic, IoT has already made its way to campuses and schools throughout the world. There are thousands of sites that offer different courses, and you can access it from any part of the world. For millions of students with special needs IOT could provide benefits from educational resources that might not be available in classroom settings. Some of the examples are that there are mobile apps to help students with reading disabilities to read and understand materials, there are voice recognition apps that helps students with writing difficulties, and so on. The booming of IoT in education is adding more resources or tools to the classroom physically or online. Computers offer interactive audiovisual mediums and other software to present information in an effective and interesting way [[11](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_011)]. Through online platforms “study anywhere anytime” is a new form of phrase accepted by people all around the world especially during this pandemic time. No matter where you are in the world you just got to have Internet connection and a digital device, and a whole world of knowledge opens up to you. It is easily possible to learn a new skill or subject online. The novel corona time has made people use these opportunities to the maximum of their abilities.

Creating IoT products can be a great help to provide an easy environment for students, also reflected in [Figure 3](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_fig_003) [[12](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_012)]:

*   Digital highlighters which scan any printed text and transfer it to your device making the note-taking process incredibly fast and easy.

*   Radio frequency identification (RFID) chips can be used to track any physical object, for example,, plants and animals, in order to take up information and store them in the cloud for later analysis and inference.

*   QR codes which can be embedded in books to access any additional resources or students can embed them in their offline work which can be linked to an online portfolio of the project.

*   Smart classrooms – IoT will make classrooms of the future a tech haven for the teachers as well as students. Augmented reality (AR) helps students to give detailed information on any object they are studying on. For example, classes will be able to use textbooks encoded with AR possibilities. These types of techs have the ability to immerse students in a completely new environment. Students have the ability to explore anything up close from heavy machineries to human body and all the way to underwater ecosystem [[13](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_013)].

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_003.png)

Figure 3: IoT education application.

Creating smart classrooms and different other new technologies for the purpose of educational and learning opportunities through multimedia and networking systems has helped teachers and the administration departments to interact and keep track of students individually or as a group, specifically during the lockdown period as it was hard to reach out to students without proper traditional classrooms. The content presented as optimization, interaction, and convenience of learning resources in smart classrooms is an advanced technical concept.

Cloud computing technology is a type of Internet-based computing. Server storage and applications are various services that are delivered over the Internet to the organization’s computers and devices. Correspondingly 5G offers countless benefits to the management by offering opportunities for work and entertainment in the clouds to take advantage of cloud computing technology. This skill permits the reduction of the cost of computing application hosting, content storage, and delivery. Different cloud computing models are critical to getting the most out of the services offered by 5G intelligent classroom education systems. The ultra-reliable high-speed 5G waiting time is a catalyst for the application of this service [[14](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_014)].

Similarly, wearable device are the ones which help in students’ issues by monitoring their movements in and out. These gadgets are connected to the management’s central digital platform. Through these automations, it is not even necessary to take attendance of students as these devices automatically detect whether the student is present or absent. If at all the parents are worried regarding the whereabouts of their child, these technologies also might help them to check the student’s location and their actions by sending them messages [[15](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_015)]. In combination with the IoT censors, these devices only allow the authorized people to access the door of the building and so when an unauthorized person arrives, the access is denied.

Likewise, further example is a report automation which is connected device that enables the school administrators to take advantage of AI to produce their weekly, monthly, or yearly reports on a wide range of issues, including student progress reports, financial planning reports, and reports for the school board and more.

### 2.3 Future of IoT

The future of IoT industry is seen as a huge success. According to *Business Insider* intelligence estimation 24 billion devices following the path of IoT will be installed by 2020 and IoT revenue will reach around 357 billion in the upcoming years which also results in a lot of job opportunities in the IT industry. As we move from www (static pages web) to web2 (social networking web) to web3 (ubiquitous computing web), the need for data-on-demand using sophisticated intuitive queries increases significantly. With the rise of cloud-based technologies along with data mining and big data analytics, the future of IoT seems to be promising and with it, the applications it is going to provide [[16](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_016)].

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_004.jpg)

Figure 4: Development of IoT.

IoT ([Figure 4](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_fig_004)) is going to expand at a very rapid rate and with it there are going to be huge advancements in every field. We can leverage the power of IoT to implement smart learning environment that facilitates better learning and greater retention rates. This advancement in education can produce better individuals in terms of skills and knowledge.

## 3 Artificial intelligence

The technology and innovations of fourth industrial revolution have rapidly boomed and revolutionized the processes of this planet. Artificial Intelligence, or AI, has been the driving force behind the Industry 4.0\. AI is the machine replication of human intelligence through application of algorithms and development of a computing environment. Designed by computer scientists, AI enhances and imitates human capabilities in the form of machine learning and performs functions similar to us like, planning, reasoning, learning, and problem-solving. Research surrounding AI is concerned with developing machines and codes to automate daily tasks which require certain intelligence along with presenting solutions to real-life problems. AI systems are constantly being developed and used in different fields of business, military, medicine and military [17].

In regular household applications, AI is being applied as digital assistants in our mobile phones and smart home devices through Siri and Alexa which work on the popular machine learning software. Much of people’s interactions online in chat boxes are powered by AI software that recognizes customer queries and responds based on their knowledge. Search engines for Bing, Google, and so on are operated through algorithms created through AI systems; these examples demonstrate the depth of human interaction with AI machines which has drastically increased in the past few years. Much of the manufacturing and business processes also depend on smooth functioning of AI and its understanding of user interface [[18](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_018)].

Natural learning processing (NLP) and machine learning are the most employed forms of AI that are used for image recognition, deep learning and planning, content extraction and text generation. Speech component of AI is highly utilized for machine translation and speech to text/text to speech recognition. Big organizations deploy these AI systems in different areas of working, the highest for external communication (71%), followed by customer interaction (66%), and workforce management and security (60%). AI-powered industries are expected to gain 30% more annually compared to their non-AI competitors [[19](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_019)].

### 3.1 Current trends of AI

Experts suggest AI would dominate the future of the world, especially considering the growing trends of machine learning software and AI skills in developed and developing countries. *Forbes* reports AI skills would penetrate the United States 100%, China 92%, and India 84% in the next 10 years of their development (as shown in [Figure 5](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_fig_005)). Similarly, AI leaders of global tech companies suggest the next five years will have a big impact on the machine learning/Artificial Intelligence models created for enhancing processes due to regulation of data transmission and privacy [[20](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_020)]. Latest research also reports about 86% businesses running on AI models are deriving benefits through enhanced customer experience due to better understanding of certain human requirements and 25% AI-adopted business are expecting to see an increase in user-base and revenue in coming years [[21](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_021)].

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_005.png)

Figure 5: Prediction of artificial intelligence skills.

The 2020 pandemic has been especially important for the AI industry as the changes during this period have uncovered the true value of ML software through enhanced planning for workforce, customer retention, and simulation modelling. The growing trends of application of AI in augmenting and automating business processes in 2020 will continue as adopters of such technology report improvement of existing services and products.

In keeping with the transformations of Industry 4.0, many companies are also being forced to adopt AI-based solutions during this pandemic to address their business needs and obtain flexible solutions through advanced analytics and machine learning [[22](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_022)]. The education sector was deeply affected by the seismic shifts of the sudden worldwide lockdowns; all the stakeholders in a school/university/institute adopted applications (Zoom and Microsoft teams) with AI features to continue the learning process for students. Likewise, the pandemic has also introduced the need for emotion AI software equipped with the capability to understand complex human emotions and cognitive states through identifying vocal and facial expressions of users [[23](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_023)].

### 3.2 Role of AI in education industry

Artificial Intelligence and machine learning have long begun to alter the education industry, and the recent advancements in the technology have made it possible to increase access and personalize learning for the students. The various applications of the AI systems is changing the way students are learning and understanding, while making education easily accessible through computers and smart devices for all students especially for those unable to attend physical class. Such technology is impacting the learning process from playschool through advanced education, by offering better opportunities to enhance student experience with adaptive teaching features and personalized tools [[24](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_024)]. Other than learners, AI models are supporting teachers with managing tedious tasks and aiding education management bodies with automating administrative duties.

Specific software were designed during the Education 4.0 revolution through ML and AI modeling that have helped significantly improve a student’s learning within and outside a classroom. Digital content can now be created by AI robots for classrooms; virtual interfaces can be developed using such robots for customized learning for students. AI system–powered application “Cram101” condenses contents in various textbooks and provides more digestible material via study guides, flashcards, and practice tests for students. AI technologies would now allow individualized learning to be prioritized by teachers by offering tools for differentiation in classrooms. Companies like Carnegie Learning are developing tutoring systems for students with specific needs and learning disabilities to offer lessons, tests, and instant feedback by using student provided data to identify knowledge gaps and create specific materials. Such applications are still in early testing stages, but soon could work as completely informed digital professors with broad array of learning styles to help students [[25](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_025)] (as displayed in [Figure 6](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_fig_006)). Machine learning tech and predictive analytics are being explored to create and update content for textbooks, curriculum, and materials provided to students, in order to provide only current information and curate substance for distinct learning curves. AI drives applications like Grammarly, Turnitin, and Kahoot to evaluate shared information and provide instant feedback to the learners. Immediate feedback given with resources to improve child’s knowledge can increase their interest and motivation to study [[26](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_026)].

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_006.png)

Figure 6: Benefits of AI for education.

Virtual reality and augmented reality technology can be combined with AI to create modernized digital learning environment for students with stories, characters, and 3D gaming that make understanding a truly unique experience. Tools and online applications could create openly available, global classrooms for students with disabilities/physical impairments, or for those speaking different language. This opens up learning opportunities for children in remote parts of the world or those unable to attend classes due to illness like, during the pandemic [[27](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_027)]. AI could essentially bridge the gap between learning and accessibility of classrooms for learners.

For educators, AI has opened up a plethora of possibilities from reducing workload to improving class curriculums and lessons. One biggest job of teacher is grading and evaluating homework, while providing valuable feedback to students for improvement. AI applications have computerized the grading process for multiple-choice questions allowing teachers to spend personalized time with students instead [[28](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_028)]. Developers are also working on software to grade essays and written answers as well which could offer educators more time to provide adaptability and understanding during class.

Educations and lecturers have to design lessons and resources with differentiation for their students and content should be adapted to the abilities of twenty-first-century netizens. Platforms like Netex Learning permit teachers to create digital curriculum and content with online AI assistants through videos, audios, and other resources specifically designed for students with different needs [[29](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_029)]. During the pandemic, teachers were able to use many virtual AI platforms to increase student motivation in learning and improve their digital knowledge through customized training. By combining the best of teachers and technology, the goals of Education 4.0 can be achieved for best student outcomes.

Other than students and teachers, the management of schools and universities holds important responsibilities and administrative tasks that can be more efficiently accomplished with IT processes and virtual assistants powered by AI. Through modeling of previous data using ML, admin can keep track of departmental operations and obtain forecasts for better future planning. The forecasts could be used to record and maintain database of school supplies for students and food stock for cafeterias to avoid wastage in the future. AI significantly benefits the admission process through automating the classification of paperwork and enrolment process of student. This could also assist with eliminating the human bias related to admission process for fair and credible selection of future learners. AI systems can significantly improve the productivity of education institutes, while reducing the operation cost and increase their social visibility [[30](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_030)].

Researchers and scientists are exploring AI technologies to create smart virtual environments and digital platforms for students under Education 4.0\. Reports suggest that the University of Southern California are developing robots that could play the role of a teacher or virtual educator by recognizing facial cues and gestures of students and respond with tailored verbal or non-verbal cues. Applications related to digital learning and simulation are projected as one of the highest benefit market in next few years. Research analysts for global AI have stated that AI tech in education industry will witness a 45% growth from 2018 to 2023 and the US education market is expected to reach 3.68 billion USD by 2023 (shown as [Figure 7](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_fig_007)). Marketers also suggest that by 2025 about 47% of education tools would be developed using AI systems [[31](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_031)].

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_007.png)

Figure 7: Expected growth of AI in education market.

Artificial Intelligence can competently drive personalization, efficiency, and globalization of education not just for students but for teachers and administration of education bodies as well. While the world witnesses an exponential growth of AI in education as well as other industries around the world, it is crucial for stakeholders to understand the benefits and invest in adopting AI technology to create a better learning environment for students, teachers, and the administration.

## 4 Methodology

Research methodology is the process used to identify the problem and select and analyze data collected from participants to accomplish the objectives discussed in the paper [[32](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_032)]. The data was gathered using survey data collection method. This has helped us gain a better understanding of student’s experience with Education 4.0 technology. The sample for data collection consisted of 315 students (125 School and 190 University level) of mixed genders from India and Middle East. The sample was chosen on the preferred attributes using convenient sampling technique. A good rapport was established with the respondents and the instructions regarding the survey were clearly mentioned in the form. An online survey was developed with questions regarding Education 4.0 and its technologies for students, with general questions to identify their current level of education. Confidentiality of subject information was completely maintained. The data collected via the survey was analyzed and presented in the form of graphical representations.

## 5 Findings

A research survey regarding the application of Education 4.0 tools and technology during the pandemic for e-learning was developed and distributed to collect information on student’s perspective. Analysis of data from 310 participants of schools and universities indicated a positive interest in students toward the introduction of these digital tools into their learning environment. The pandemic has surely affected the traditional forms of learning, yet proper application of technology has enhanced student’s experience.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_008.png)

Figure 8: Development of Web 3.0.

For the research, the respondents were first asked about their general experience with digital classrooms during the pandemic. As shown in Chart 1, 43.4% subjects had a good experience, 23.7% had a neutral feeling, and 10.5 % had a very good experience with the shift on education onto digital platforms. Majority of the students believe the various online tools and technology used by educators during the lockdown were valuable and enhanced their experience.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_009.jpg)

Figure 9: Technologies of Web 3.0 for education.

Referring to Chart 2, we could analyze that 44.1% of the sample population agrees that the resources shared by schools and universities has made online education, an enhanced learning experience. A 15.7% of the sample claimed that the tools and resources of Education 4.0 were not useful or did not assist them to create a better learning experience for their digital classrooms during the pandemic. Yet there are 40.3% who had a neutral stand on the effectiveness resources.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_010.jpg)

Chart 1: “How would you describe your online learning experience during the pandemic?”.

From the research conducted in this chapter, certain technologies of AI and IoT were most highlighted for use in schools and universities, such specific Education 4.0 developed tools were given as options to the respondents to understand which of them were useful for their education. Results show, 63% students have used Turnitin, Grammarly, and other such AI writing assistants for their learning, 39% have been taught with or learnt using smart interactive white boards, 32% have used AI-driven MOOCs and language applications to improve their skills, and 28% have used digital and smart highlighters to make study material easier to understand (shown in Chart 3). This indicates that a lot of IoT and AI tools developed for the education sector are being utilized by students to enhance their digital learning.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_011.jpg)

Chart 2: “Do you think the resources and tools shared by schools and universities during the pandemic have made smart digital classrooms, a better learning experience?”.

Referring to Chart 4, elaborates on the expediency of Web 3.0 and its applications with online classes or during the education process. As shown 58.5% of the population responds with “they have not” found tools of Web 3.0 useful, with 15.7% taking a neutral stand on the on the usefulness of Web 3.0 and its applications. Although there are 25.9% agreeing that web 3.0 had been applied in their learning environment and assisted in their online education. This explains that not much of the population is aware about Web 3.0 and its applications or the tools have not been properly put into application for student’s education.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_012.jpg)

Chart 3: “Have you utilized any of these Education 4.0 tools for digital learning?”.

As students were inquired about their experience with digital tools for e-learning used in classrooms, it was crucial to understand from them suggestions to make their experience better as well. Majority of the responses (as shown in Chart 3) focused on making classes “more interactive with 3D/virtual environments” and providing “digestible content with variety of animation and media.” The others recommended “initiating blended learning in classrooms” and “Personalisation of lessons and improve work feedback” to improve their learning experience in schools and universities. The AI and IoT systems discussed in this chapter could be appropriately applied through different mediums to achieve positive student outcomes.

After analyzing the sample data, the findings shows that the new technologies are still not considered due to lack of awareness of the mentioned applications by the sample population. Most of the students used the newest tools of Education 4.0 for utmost involvement and improvement in their digital learning experience. From the responses, we understand that proper implementation of e-learning resources during the pandemic has enhanced the classroom environment for students as virtual classrooms and increase in digital resources for lessons were given higher preference for post-pandemic situations. The recommendations given through this research should be strongly considered by educators and school/university management to improve the overall quality of learning provided to students.

## 6 Web 3.0

Web-based education and learning has opened up a vast world through the evolution of Internet and has given us the opportunity to access incredible prospects. As shown in the [Figure 8](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_fig_008), the evolution of web-based technologies started from the outmoded version of web which was coined as web 1.0 and established itself as “read-only” medium. This was the very birth of the Internet. It is like a centralized network wherein the main server hosts a website and with different servers getting access to this website. The main drawback of such a technology was that if at all one server went offline the entire website went offline. The next generation was of the Web 2.0 which it is said to a “read/write” medium. This was the creation of enhanced Internet services which are presently used by people globally, for example Facebook, Netflix, and Google. The Web 2.0 can be explained as distributed technological systems where servers are all around and controlled by multiple data centers, where each of them will maintain a copy of the data and thus the advantage is that even if one of the data centers is offline the rest of the team would still be functional and there is no gap in availability of the application [[33](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_033)].

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_013.jpg)

Chart 4: “Have applications of Web 3.0 (like Alexa, Siri, Virtual reality, and 3D graphics) assisted you in classrooms or during the education process?”.

The current most updated version of the web is coined as Web 3.0. This newer, advanced version has no ownership and helps in rebuilding internet as the original creators intended of the internet which means freedom, censorship resistant, anyone can participate and generate a sustainable income, more competition, and lower barrier and in the present scenario it has an easier entry to the marketplace. Web 3.0 consists of a decentralized system and every node of the server is able to maintain a copy of the data and moreover each node is able to validate all of the data which means faster validation of what any person has previously added to the implementation [[34](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_034)].

One of the best examples would be bitcoin where all of the data that is processed through the application will be time-stamped. This technology is an unconventional era of automation which allows users to not only read and write but also execute and innovate their own ideas. This mechanism has allowed machines to implement reasoning and problem solving which was only expected from the human brain. Within a span of short time, the Web 2.0 and the novel Web 3.0 technology is on the verge of creating new tools to simplify work and aid each individual for facilitating web-based education and learning [[35](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_035)].

The Web has endured extensive development and is undeniably growing faster. World Wide Web (www) has been used to improve our collaboration and communication, while making exchange of resources and delivering quality content easier. The world is in the third generation of the internet and specific innovations help us better define the features most useful for us. The semantic web has enhanced our ability to search and analyze the true meaning of words and data to help us generate, connect, and share content globally. Google, Facebook, Microsoft, and other big tech giants have adopted semantic web technologies to improve their database structure and search engine responses. Another enhanced feature of Web 3.0 is virtualization through 3D graphics and high-speed connection bandwidths [[36](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_036)]. This trend used three-dimensional applications to create virtual worlds/environments for exploration like the app Second Life.

With Web 3.0 our online search options and results have excelled as well. Search engines are no longer restricted to just text-inputs but also allow use of various media objects for search inputs. For example, Google allows us to add images for our device to retrieve other images with similar features as the objects in the input; similar technology has been developed to allow use of videos and audios as well. At this stage of the Internet, developers and researchers have made a remarkable progress with technologies for wireless networks and communication. The ubiquitous devices and platforms now allow us to access and manipulate a multitude of data from anywhere and anytime, even while being on the move, like Apple smart watches which allow us to control home appliances and act as our personal assistant. Such technology has improved our quality of life and the world’s economy; with constant development, it is possible for us to reach greater possibilities virtually [[37](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_037)].

### 6.1 Applications of Web 3.0 technology in education

Over the last few years, different educational institutes have been offering online services through the web for admissions, management of activities and digital learning environments that connect students with excellent resources and each other for a better understanding. The services and tools of Web 3.0 have helped foster an open approach toward learning [[38](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_038)]. Features of technology could be carefully applied to the workings of the education sector to benefit students, teachers, and the management of schools/universities. The quality learning assets can be made advanced through ubiquitous devices and platforms, which could also be used to track the location and progress of students in their learning environment. The use of semantic web has a tremendous potential to enhance education for students, as our access to various interests and activities through our preferred channels is wide open. Educators could utilize semantic blogs and intelligent search engines to improve their education content with a variety of media and animation [[39](#9783110722642_008157.xhtml_b_9783110722789-006_ref_039)].

Similarly, a student’s digital classroom can be improved using 3D technology and augmented reality to create virtual learning environments like, labs, museums, and zoos to make understanding and collaborating an enjoyable experience. Applications like Google Virtual Tour provide students the opportunity to explore parts of outside world that may not be easily accessible. Other such technologies further applied with AI and IoT tools can create better opportunities and environments for students.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110722789-006_fig_014.jpg)

Chart 5: “If no, what do you suggest can make a student’s experience better?”.

## 7 Conclusion

This chapter explored the various trends of Education 4.0 extracted from Industrial Revolution (IR) 4.0\. Systems and models reviewed for existing literature on IoT and Artificial Intelligence have given us a greater understanding of its application in the education sector. Smart virtual assistants and school management systems powered by AI models could improve the quality of education provided. Also, applications like digital highlighters and smart white boards would enhance learning resources and methods of teaching. An open, personalized approach toward learning has been initiated through the advanced tools of Web 3.0\. The findings gathered from the questionnaire designed for a sample of 315 students regarding their experience with Education 4.0 tools gave us an overall positive perception. Students were satisfied with their digital learning experience during the pandemic and offered suggestions for interactive and blended education. Even though learners preferred returning to traditional classrooms to continue knowledge development, their experience with technology has fostered a stronger need for educators and management to inculcate digital resources for their learning.

This chapter provides a solid foundation to explore other novel technologies of Education 4.0 which need to be reviewed and studied for its application in the current education industry. Through this study we would like to establish an emphasis on providing netizen students education on proper use of technology and resources with AI and IoT for flexible, improvised skills. Future recommendations for researchers and developers would be to carefully consider the application of technology for education especially for the post-pandemic situation or future scenarios. Providing students and teachers with easy access to multiple resources and secure technology could create an improved learning experience. Proper models of AI and IoT technology should be developed in collaboration with Web 3.0 tools to explore opportunities and possibilities of the fourth education revolutions. Advanced technology accurately applied with learning can open a Pandora of prospects for learners and educators.

# Artificial Intelligence (AI)-Aided Algorithms in Medical Practice with Special Emphasis on Genetic Disease Diagnostics

Tapash RudraS. B. Goyal

#### Abstract

The modern-day medical practice has been reaching greater heights every day with the latest technological improvisations. Medical fraternity is now capable of combating the deadliest of diseases with the advent of sophisticated tools and techniques. However, some sections of medical world remain obscure in regard to the diagnosis, prognosis, and therapeutics. Genetic or rare diseases are the crucial among them. Unlike the commonly known disorders, genetic diseases are quite unique primarily due to their association with the human genome. Moreover, scarcity of authentic data, restricted number of patients across the global periphery, and lack of adequate diagnostic measures are equally contributing to the hidden facets around such diseases. Therefore, it is imperative to have new and improvised options in order to find the unexplored facts related to them. Artificial intelligence and machine learning algorithms have appreciably enlightened various wings of rare disorders including the diagnosis. However, to make this new technology a powerful weapon, sizeable efforts are required bearing in mind genomic data and conventional methods cannot be the sole way out toward the improvement of genetic disease research and management.

Keywords: Artificial Intelligence, Algorithm, Machine Learning, Genetic Disease, Diagnostics,

## 1 Overview

Human beings have been evolving since the first day on earth in tandem with the human civilization itself. The sustenance of humankind, however, continued to march on with the roller coaster ride. Our ancient ancestors were used to be intimidated by voracious carnivores and periodical natural calamities until coincidentally they managed to ignite the fire by generating the friction among the sharp edges of the stones. That was the beginning of the technology and it is beyond doubt that is the biggest boom we, human beings, have in the repertoire.

As the juggernaut moves on we find ourselves more toward the technology-driven era. The ongoing global COVID-19 pandemic has been humongous by far, in relevance to the overall collapse of the global integrity. To overhaul the outcome of such a colossal event we must come up with ideas those hitherto been thought about.

With the advent of new and sophisticated technologies medical science has been galloping to a new orbit. However, since the last few decades the implication of artificial intelligence (AI) has added a completely new dimension to it. The influence of various AI methods put a dilemma whether the natural or artificial intelligence is the potential roadmap to the global healthcare system. To be honest, diagnosis of diseases leading to the successive treatments is not replaceable but this modern boom of technology can enormously facilitate the healthcare professionals to ascertain the verdict of a disease. At the same time, the modern AI-directed analytics has opened up a new window to dissect the huge number of clinical data into relatively easier forms. This has made the task easier for the healthcare professionals and clinicians to be sure about the decision-making aspects [[1](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_001), [2](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_002), [3](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_003), [4](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_004), [5](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_005)].

### 1.1 Artificial intelligence in medical research: an account of current scenario

The holy grail of AI is the interpretation of human beings as the machines. To add to it, AI-based machineries are tailor-made to materialize the general theories of intelligence in agents. Biological entities such as humans and animals are treated beyond their limits in a wider perspective to maximize the outreach of the possible outcome. The notion of AI is extremely enterprising; at the same time, it is a force to be reckoned with to tackle the philosophical doubts those have been existing since more than thousand years.

We are heading more toward the Industrial Revolution 4.0 which is the narrative of fast-paced innovative development by securing greater efficacy in line with digitalized supply chain instead of the conventional one. Medical science is one of the potential thrust areas as far as the applications of Industry 4.0 are concerned. Improvised implants, techniques, and modern devices are some of the significant attributes. Moreover, Internet of Things (IoT) and Internet of Services has excelled the overall progression in relevance to the healthcare sector in the modern days [[6](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_006)]. Another striking aspect of Industry 4.0 is the replacement of conventional computing by means of quantum or cloud computing to propel the efficacy by several folds. This is very much noteworthy as in today’s medical world the implication of quantum computing has reduced the cost to a considerable extent. Furthermore, use of sophisticated AI sensors has also escalated the productivity and efficiency among the various machines. The mentioned concept is a handful especially in strategizing the clinical practice [[7](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_007), [8](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_008)].

#### 1.1.1 Significance of Industry 4.0 in medical fraternity

The principal features of Industry 4.0 that make it significantly different from the previous three are the multidisciplinary approach, automation, and flexibility. In combination with these modernized facets Industrial Revolution 4.0 seems to be the exact weapon to have in the repertoire of the medical practitioners across the world, keeping in mind the days to come after the ongoing pandemic [[8](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_008), [9](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_009), [10](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_010), [11](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_011)].

#### 1.1.2 Key elements that make Industry 4.0 extremely relevant in medical sciences:

Optimize the efficacy and productivity [[12](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_012), [13](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_013)]

Interpret the patient data of a huge proportion with minimal cost [[14](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_014), [15](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_015)]

Analyze the data with maximum accuracy [[16](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_016)]

Deliver the best quality [[6](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_006), [7](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_007)]

Improve both material and tool management [[17](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_017), [18](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_018)]

Maintain the clinical data in sophisticated computer-aided design (CAD) file instead of keeping in inventories [[19](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_019), [20](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_020)]

Produce customized implants in line with the patient requisite by developing smart AI-based sensors [[21](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_021), [22](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_022)]

Implement proper aids to sharpen the surgical interventions [[23](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_023), [24](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_024)]

With the advent of digital technology and sensor machineries the new diseases are autonomously detected with ease [[25](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_025), [26](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_026)]

Organize the centralized information database in the hospitals and other healthcare avenues [[27](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_027), [28](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_028), [29](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_029)]

Depict the medical data, relate with the relevant patient information [[30](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_030), [31](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_031)]

## 2 AI-based research/publication profiling in the recent past in line with the medical sciences: a brief outlook

AI-aided research and subsequent publications in relevance to the various wings of medical science have been one of the emerging fields since the last few years. Global publication databases such as *Scopus* and *Web of Science* already indexed round about 70 scholarly articles from 2013 to 2020 on the mentioned agenda. It is worth mentioning that in the context of ongoing global crisis the AI-based research in line with medical aspect becomes more significant as it portrays to substantial logic into that.

It began with the publication of the solitary article on the mentioned subject in 2013, with no further addition to that in 2014 and 2015\. However, the number of original research significantly increased afterward; in the year 2016 there were six articles followed by another fourteen in 2017 in *Scopus* and *Web of Science*. It looked more promising as in 2018 the database showed another 34 research papers on the concerned area. By the year 2020 the trend continued to be in the right direction, with significant contributions coming from the AI-based research in tandem with the contemporary medical issues ([Table 1](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_tab_001)).

Table 1:Frequency of AI-based research publications in line with medical practice in global databases from 2013 to 2020.

| 2. | Decision science | 11 |
| 3. | Mathematics | 11 |

### 2.1 Web 2.0 and today’s medical world: the making of a new era

The concept of Web 2.0 is not entirely convincing. It has created significant dilemma among the web experts across the globe. However, the concept has still been a pinnacle as far as the exchange of medical ideas are concerned [[32](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_032)]. According to the medical librarians Web 2.0 is the standout across the wings of the advanced medical world [[33](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_033)]. The implications of Web 2.0 becomes prominent in the context of the ongoing global pandemic where the work flow needs a continuous refinement and creative thinking in order to cope up with the norms of the new normal. Indeed, the medical research paid dividends over the last decades or so with the advent of Web 2.0, especially, during the transformation from Web 1.0 to Web 2.0 [[34](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_034), [35](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_035)]. Since the introduction of “digital narratives” the growth of Web 2.0 has been exponential in the field of biological sciences [[36](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_036), [37](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_037), [38](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_038), [39](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_039), [40](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_040), [41](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_041), [42](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_042), [43](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_043), [44](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_ref_044)] ([Table 2](#9783110722642_009283.xhtml_b_9783110722789-007_tab_002)).

Table 2:Applications of Web 2.0 in medical fraternity.

| Application | Designated web portal | Significance |
