It denotes both a distinctive training method and specific content area. | Hinrichs [[51](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_051)]; Seashore [[52](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_052)] |
| 5 | Role-playing method | In role-playing an individual learns by watching, introspecting, correcting, or improvising the information obtained and by participating in the general assessment of the complete activity. | Sogunro [[53](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_053)]; Johnson & Johnson [[54](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_054)] |
| 6 | Business games method | Business games are a method of using experiential techniques to develop leaders. | Lopes et al. [[64](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_064)]; Faria [[65](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_065)] |
| 7 | Movie films method | Numerous research have indicated that videos embedded with training or education module have a huge impact in learning in the fields of psychology, counseling, medicine, management, and political science. | Borko and Pittman [[68](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_068)]; Brophy [[69](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_069)]; LeFevre [[70](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_070)]; Moreno & Valdez [[71](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_071)]; Pryor & Bitter [[72](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_072)]; Richardson & Kile [[73](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_073)]; Seago [[74](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_074)]; Sherin [[75](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_075)]; Wang & Hartley [[76](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_076)] |
| 8 | Conference (discussion) method | In conference (discussion) method, there is exchange of ideas, and these ideas are expanded, criticized, and honed. Also, for such discussions, the capacity to listen is an important skill. | Smith & Foley [[82](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_082)]; Costin [[81](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_081)] |
| 9 | Television lecture method | Television is a strong interaction tool with tremendous potential for informing, entertaining, and educating. Advanced mass communication technology presents teachers with the potential in the area of teaching and learning for a scientific revolution. | Jayakumar [[84](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_084)]; McIsaac & Guanwardena [[86](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_086)] |

***Lecture method:*** The lecture method was the only way to transfer knowledge from one generation to another before the industrial revolution, when books were not yet mass commodities [[42](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_042)]. One of the most common methods of training is the lecture method. Lecture method is an easy, quick, and inexpensive way to present the vast problems to many groups of learners [[43](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_043)]. However, the cons of lecture method also need to be looked up, such as long lectures, one-way communication which leads to forgetting of issues [[44](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_044)]. Lecture method is a traditional method to illustrate information. It encompasses verbal presentation on a subject by a qualified person [[45](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_045)]. Research depicts that lecture method is more effective when it comes to acquiring knowledge; however, learning is enhanced by 10% via programmed instruction in comparison to lecture method.

***Programmed instruction method:*** Programmed instruction is a method of providing trainees or students with a graded series of guided steps for new subject matter. These trainees or students work by themselves at their own pace through the programmed material and test their understanding after each move by answering a question or filling in a diagram [[46](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_046)]. New subject matter/knowledge/skill can be presented through programmed instruction method in a sequence of controlled steps. Trainees have the flexibility and space to go through the programmed materials at their own pace stepwise, and each step they are tested through examination question or filling a diagram. Instruction, whether aimed at developing academic skills in retarded children, increasing general knowledge in normal children; or advancing mathematical sophistication in gifted youngsters, may be conceived of as a process in which a teacher systematically and effectively arranges and rearranges an environment to bring about desired behavioral changes. Teaching on this basis, in whole or part, has been called as programmed instruction [[15](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_015), [47](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_047)]. Programmed instruction is a technology based on experimental analysis of behavior [[11](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_011), [14](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_014), [16](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_016)].

***Case study method:*** In case study method, the trainees are required to read and retain information independently from a particular case study; case studies are interactive in nature [[48](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_048)]. Case study method is the best method to improve problem-solving skills [[49](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_049)]. Case study helps in enhancing of decision-making skills, enhances communication and interpersonal skills, and also strengthens analytical skills. This training method is ideal for a situation where the learners have some prerequisite information and could benefit from the trainings essence of applicability. This is a low-risk, low-cost, training method that can concurrently train a large number of learners, because of which it is a popular choice for law, medicine, and counseling students training [[50](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_050)]. Carroll et al. (1972) [[10](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_010)] did a survey of industry-based trainers, and it was found that case study method was the second best method for knowledge acquisition; the best method was programmed instruction method. Also, for knowledge retention, case study method is the best training method after programmed instruction method as per the literature.

***Sensitivity training method:*** Sensitivity training method model is a group gathering without a plan to discuss topics relating to “here and now” of the group procedure [[51](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_051)]; it denotes both a distinctive training method and a specific content area. The objective of sensitivity training is classified into three categories: individual point of view, group point of view, and the organization point of view. Individual point of view: A trainee can get a clear picture of how he/she is perceived of any given act. Different individuals have different views of the same behavior. For example, to know the effect on any person as supportive or against it, meaningful or meaningless, clear or ambiguous. It is not very common for all components of a group to have the same view of any particular person or event [[52](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_052)].

***Role-playing method:*** In role-playing, an individual learns by watching, introspecting, correcting, or improvising the information obtained and by participating in the general assessment of the complete activity [[53](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_053)]. The more authentic the role-playing and more successful the practice, the more you will feel emotional engagement, and the more you will understand [[54](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_054)]. Role-playing as a training method involves playing a role as a method that is used in individual and group circumstances such as counseling, interviews, customer service and sales, private interactions, negotiations, government meetings, team committees, research, and procedures of change that involve a lot of practical involvement of the participants, in the work [[55](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_055), [56](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_056), [57](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_057), [58](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_058), [59](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_059), [60](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_060)]. Role-playing has the advantage of letting trainees actually practice relevant skills and thus has greater transferability to the workplace than do didactic lectures of abstract discussions about leadership [[61](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_061)]. Role-playing is particularly suitable for offering trainees the opportunity to practice and focus together on how to handle important responsibilities in their work, or for sensitivity training and other purposes that include attitude training [[62](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_062)]. Also, a research exploring the use of role-playing in education of genetic counseling students found that participants saw this approach as useful in educating them on the genetic counseling system and consequences, and this experience would help them interact with patients effectively [[63](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_063)].

***Business games method:*** Business games method is a process of using experiential techniques to develop leaders [[64](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_064)]. Business games are widely accepted in management education or training. Around 90% AACSB (Association to Advance Collegiate Schools of Business) schools are using business games as a management simulation for management education [[65](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_065)]. A review of 304 business simulation education and learning articles shows that the five aspects gained through business games are strategy, decision-making, teamwork, experience, and learning outcomes and objectives [[66](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_066)]. Business games as a training method epitomizes experiential learning [[67](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_067)]. A business game is a tool to test the understanding of theoretical insights through practical application.

***Movie films method:*** Numerous researches have indicated that videos embedded with training or education module have a huge impact in learning in the field of psychology, counseling, medicine, management, and political science [[68](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_068), [69](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_069), [70](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_070), [71](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_071), [72](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_072), [73](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_073), [74](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_074), [75](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_075), [76](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_076)]. Learning through pictures, that is, videos and audiovisual, is a superior learning as compared to verbal, that is, audio conditions. This is related to the picture superiority effect [[77](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_077), [78](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_078)]. In order to strengthen important concepts and ideas, video clips are used as they induce visual stimuli. Generally, information is often stored in visual form such as movie clips; pictures and movie clips can be very vital to help students remember crucial ideas and get back from their long-term memory [[79](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_079)]. Around 94% of adults in Malaysia were engaged in acquisition of knowledge by watching television, video, and films [[80](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_080)].

***Conference (discussion) method:*** In conference (discussion) method a trainee and a trainer holds one-to-one discussions on the subject matter to be imparted or taught. Discussion method is beneficial for speech courses for students with low ability; also it is a good technique that helps in recalling facts. Relatively discussion may be more effective method of teaching as compared to lecture method [[81](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_081)]. In conference (discussion) method, there is an exchange of ideas and these ideas are expanded, criticized, and honed. Also, for such discussions, the capacity to listen is an important skill [[82](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_082)]. According to Johnson and Smith, 1953 [[83](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_083)], students in discussion class in a basic psychology course not only expressed opinions about the course when compared to other students who were taught the same course via lecture method, but also, these students who studied through discussion or conference technique were more open to tests and grading procedures.

***Television lecture method**:*** The television lectures are unique in a way as it includes communication between two or more locations where instructions are given at the moment specified (instructions that take place more than once at the same moment). Television is a strong interaction tool with tremendous potential for informing, entertaining, and educating. Advanced mass communication technology presents teachers with the potential in the area of teaching and learning for a scientific revolution [[84](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_084)]. Researchers have constantly discovered that television is capable of motivating and captivating learners and stimulating interest in the learning process [[85](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_085)]. Distance learning through interactive television lectures is one of the fastest-growing instructional medium [[86](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_086)]. Distance learning is not a new concept while videotaped lectures have been used for long in universities and professional courses for over two decades [[87](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_087)].

## 7 Methodology

A questionnaire was constructed on the latest effective training methods used to enhance learning of KWs in outsourcing organizations and administered to trainers, managers, senior managers, and learning and development employees who have been conducting training sessions or programs in outsourcing organizations. The questionnaire was designed in such a manner that for every training objective, three training methods were provided to respondents and they were asked to rank these three training methods for the specific training objective. These three training methods were derived from literature review, which is considered to be best for that specific training objective.

## 8 Use of Web 3.0 in training knowledge workers

Web 3.0 refers to a third wave of Internet-based services that collectively form what could be called “the intelligent web” [[88](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_088)]. In simple terms, Web 3.0 is indeed a technically advanced medium that allows users to read, write, and execute, and furthermore it enables machines to perform which earlier was expected from humans only [[89](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_089)]. It is needless to say that millions across the globe use Internet or the web to access information to learn. Web 3.0 is basically the third generation in web evolution. In order to understand the scope of Web 3.0 better we have to understand Web 1.0 and Web 2.0\. Web 1.0 was a medium in which knowledge was built with text and photographs and could be published in static form. It represented an atmosphere in which data and knowledge were static and presented without interaction between the user and the information and limited content creators, also known as read-only web [[90](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_090)]. Web 2.0 is basically an expansion of Web 1.0’s initial objectives, values, and underlying technology [[91](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_091)]. Web 2.0 has numerous benefits particularly the way in which it contributes to the development of complex knowledge networks by allowing users to engage in the web infrastructure itself, creating vast quantities of data with many possible uses. It has also contributed to the growth of user centric flexible applications [[92](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_092)].

Semantic web-based education (SWBE) is a platform to perform activities such as teaching, learning, collaboration, assessment, and other educational operations. Pedagogical representatives promote learning by engaging with learners and teachers and working with other representatives in an interactive learning atmosphere to enable content and information to flow. In SWBE, educational material focused on the web is described by learning objects. Learning objects are digital entities that can be reused for a learning resource such as electronic texts, interactive content, animations, and pictures [[93](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_093)].

***Web 3.0*** ***in education and research:*** Web 2.0 learning emphasizes the active engagement of internet users and the connection between virtual communities through social networking tools or social applications such as blog, wiki, social book marking, and social networking. A more open approach to learning will be facilitated by resources and services of Web 3.0 technologies. Web 3.0 is a combination of high-powered graphics and textual data. According to ASTD (American Society for Training & Development) President and CEO Tony Bingham, content finds you on the semantic web instead of actively pursuing it. Your actions and preferences would then decide what you find and it will be presented to your channel of choice and how you would like it [[89](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_089)]. Various e-learning resources such as MOODLE, Web 2.0, Web 3.0, and Blackboard are available that can be used in higher education. E-learning tools can offer preparation and higher education to many students who come from diverse cultural backgrounds and have varying levels of higher education. E-learning 3.0 is one of the main trends for social learning using Web 3.0 tools. On the internet, it is also simple to quickly integrate new services into existing software such as integration of Wiki with Web 3.0\. Web 3.0 has paved the evolution of e-learning and it entails lower costs relative to conventional learning and is seen as more efficient learning, hence facilitating diffusion of new processes and techniques which in turn reduces geographical challenges [[94](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_094)]. Personalization offers education plans customized to the participants based on an overview of the interests of the learners current skills and knowledge status, expectations for learning styles, as well as continuous process tracking [[95](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_095)]. The RDF, XML, and ontology-based Web 3.0 approach is ideal for personalizing learning objectives [[96](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_096)].

***Web 3.0*** ***in learning and training in organizations:*** Web 3.0 has a lot of scope in organizational learning, yet it has not been used to its maximum potential yet. Web 3.0 innovations provide smarter searches with better and more focused performance and higher quality of knowledge acquisition in a shorter period. While the immersive interactive elements of Web 3.0 have been used for years in the field of learning in the corporate training and development sector with the use of virtual environments and simulations but other immersive technologies still remain underutilized. Individuals who are oriented on the role of Web 3.0 in the learning function and in IT correlates with both a good learning function and high market efficiency [[97](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_097)]. Some of the ways Web 3.0 technologies affect and influence learning today and in future primarily in high-performing organizations are:

*   High-performing organizations are required to have divisions of learning and training open to new technologies and use these technologies to solve business problems.

*   In the next few years, the use of virtual environments, simulations, augmented reality and multiplayer gaming technology for training in organizations is anticipated to grow substantially.

*   It is time to be strategic and ready to transition with the seismic change from Web 2.0 to Web 3.0 for learning and training practitioners.

## 9 Findings and analysis

A questionnaire was designed and administered to knowledge workers working in the outsourcing industry. These knowledge workers comprised trainers, managers, senior managers, and learning and development professionals who had a fair amount of knowledge of these methods and their outcome. For every training objective, three training methods were provided to respondents and they were asked to rank these three training methods for the specific training objective. These three training methods were derived from literature review, which are considered to be best for that specific training objective.

For training objective “acquisition of new knowledge or skill,” programmed instruction was ranked first followed by case study and conference (discussion) method. Training objectives such as change in attitude, according to respondents can be best addressed by sensitivity training followed by role playing and conference (discussion) method. Case study method was ranked first followed by business games and role-playing method to address training method problem-solving skills/troubleshooting skills. In order to address training objective enhancement of interpersonal skills/people management skills, sensitivity training method was ranked first while role-playing and conference (discussion) methods were ranked second and third respectively. The final training objective retention of knowledge can be best addressed by programmed instruction method followed by case study and sensitivity training method as per the respondents (Table 4).

Table 4:Training methods ranking as per respondents.

| Training objective | Training methods | Rank |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Acquiring new knowledge or skill | Programmed instruction | 1 |
| Case study | 2 |
| Conference (discussion) | 3 |
| Change in attitude | Sensitivity training | 1 |
| Role-playing | 2 |
| Conference (discussion) | 3 |
| Problem-solving skills/troubleshooting skills | Case study | 1 |
| Business games | 2 |
| Role-playing | 3 |
| Enhancement of interpersonal skills/people management skills | Sensitivity training | 1 |
| Role-playing | 2 |
| Conference (discussion) | 3 |
| Retention of knowledge | Programmed instruction | 1 |
| Case study | 2 |
| Sensitivity training | 3 |

The respondents belonging to the age group of 25 to 34 years were 55.93%; 35 to 44 years 37.29%, 45 to 54 years 3.39%, and respondents under the age of 25 years were 3.39%. The classification and percentage of respondent’s designation were: Trainer 25%, Training Manager 20%, Assistant Manager 16.67%, Team Leader 10%, Quality Manger 6.67%, General Manager 5%, Associate General Manager 5%, Operations Manager was 5%, Executive 5%, and Associate Vice President 1.67%. Respondents whose primary role is training were 58.33% followed by quality at 18.33%, and operations at 23.33%. From a qualification perspective maximum respondent’s were graduates which was 60% followed by post graduates at 36.67% and undergraduates at 3.33%. Respondents who had more than 10 years of work experience constituted 61.67% and between 6 years to 10 years constituted 21.67%, and respondents who had work experience less than 5 years constituted 16.67%.

The respondents were asked, “How rigorous are their organization’s training programmes?” The responses were: A great deal – 33.33%; A lot – 38.33%; A moderate amount – 18.33%; A little – 8.33%; and None at all – 1.67%. The respondents were asked to “List some of the Training Needs Identified in outsourcing organizations.” The responses to this question were: Acquiring new knowledge or skill – 81.67%; Change in attitude – 43.33%; Problem-solving skills – 75.00%; Interpersonal skills – 53.33%; Knowledge retention – 53.33% and others were 10.00%. “Others” according to respondents constituted such as leadership training, client interaction and business communication, and resilience and customer centricity. The respondents opined that acquiring new knowledge or skill, problem-solving skills as critical training needs followed by interpersonal skills and knowledge retention.

A total of 48.33% of the respondents “strongly agree” that proper training methods are used to address the training needs that have been identified whereas 45% of the respondents “agree” that proper training methods are used to address the training needs that have been identified; and a total of 5% “disagree” that proper training methods are used to address the training needs that have been identified. While only 1.67% of the respondents “neither agree nor disagree.” Role-playing method and programmed instruction method are widely used as training methods in the outsourcing services sector and their usage were denoted as 78.33% and 76.67% respectively; followed by case study method which is at 71.67%, conference (discussion) method at 66.67%, lecture method at 60%, business games at 55%, sensitivity training method at 53.33%, TV lecture method 45%, movie film method at 45%, and others (online portal) constituted only 1.67%.

## 10 Conclusion

The current training methods used in practice in the outsourcing services sector with respect to addressing a particular training need from a KW’s perspective is somewhat similar to findings of Carroll et al. 1972 [[10](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_010)]. According to Carroll et al. 1972 [[10](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_010)], “Knowledge acquisition” could be best addressed by programmed instruction training method and our findings in the outsourcing services sector depict the same; that is, “acquisition of new knowledge or skill” could be best addressed by programmed instruction training method. “Change in attitude,” according to Carroll et al. [[10](#9783110722642_015421.xhtml_b_9783110722789-011_ref_010)], could be best addressed by sensitivity training method, which is similar to our finding. Training need “problem solving skills,” is best addressed by case study training method, followed by business games and role-playing method. “Knowledge retention” is best addressed by programmed instruction method followed by case study method and sensitivity training method.

Our findings state that role-playing training method is the most used training method in the outsourcing services sector. It is widely accepted and used to tackle training needs pertaining to “change in attitude” and “interpersonal skills.” Programmed instruction training method is the second most popular training method in the outsourcing services sector. Case study training method is the third most popular training method in the outsourcing services sector. Case study as a training method was found to be the best to address training need “problem solving skill.” Programmed instruction as a training method is considered to be best for the training need “acquiring new knowledge or skill and knowledge retention.”

## 11 Scope for further study

Further studies can be specifically done on role-playing and business games training methods. There exists a very limited amount of literature when compared to other training methods. Also, role-playing has emerged as the second best training method in order to address training need “change in attitude” and “interpersonal skills” as per literature review. Also, our findings ranked role-playing training method as the third best training method to address training need “problem-solving skills” and second best training method to address “interpersonal skills.” Business games as a training method is used in simulation exercises in managerial training and also it has its uses in academic training. Hence, further studies would definitely expand the body of knowledge pertaining to these two training methods.

This research received no specific grant from any funding agency in the public, commercial, or not-for-profit sectors.
